In person, virtual and phone can all be captured as long as a business discussion has taken place (i.e., not just setting up an appointment).  If there is uncertainty around what constitutes a business discussion, you can use the categories in the drop down box when entering the call as a guide.

Jordana Weiss <jordana.weiss@insmed.com>
2025-09-05 02:24 PM
Also, with regards to earlier question, there is not a special ICD 10 code for NCFBE...there are only general BE codes. That is why it is helpful for HCP to note that it is non-cystic fibrosis bronchiechtasis. As one pulmonologist made the comment to me, they are not used to having to make this distinction because most CF patients are typically seen by providers that specialize in treating CF (or have a CF clinic.)

For those who may have Ambetter in their states they may deny for the following reasons:         1) Prescribed indication is not FDA approved                                                                                    2) Failure of 2 formulary agents (must be in the same therapeutic class) (If no brand name drug pt must use generic or biosimilar version)  3) member must try alternate drugs..

For anyone with Mayo clinics- I had a meeting today with the head pharmacist and PA lead for pulmonary division. All Mayo PA’s across the country go through this central PA team. They have been pleasantly surprised by how many prescriptions have been approved when include CT scan and chart notes, appreciated the PA checklist and other resources available on website. If anyone has questions about their process, feel free to reach out one off.
Message edited: Tisha Williams <tisha.williams@insmed.com>
2025-09-19 12:24 PM
Greetings all, not to sure if this scenario has been discussed, however I wanted to share. Yesterday, I encountered situation where an EF was received from a PA-C and the SP (Maxor) wouldn't accept his signature.  After some research with my IC, it was explained by Maxor that since the  dispensing pharmacy is in Texas, the state's law requires a supervising physician on file for physician assistants and nurse practitioners who are prescribing medication.  If a prescription comes in written by a NP/PA, then Maxor will treat it like a prescription clarification.  They will call or fax to verify the supervising MD.  Sometimes the office will send in a new prescription from that supervising MD but that isn’t needed.  A verification or a notation from MD or RPH fufills the requirement.  If they can’t verify the prescription, Maxor will send it to the Bogart, Ga location to be filled.  ** Just wanted to share **
Marlene Saitta <Marlene.Saitta@Insmed.com>
2025-09-19 12:31 PM
Thank you for the details on this Tisha!

Justin Gaskins <justin.gaskins@insmed.com>

Just a FYI to everyone.  If your account is completing a Tricare PA, be sure they fill out the PA from along with the New Innovator Drug form.  I had a case sitting and the I worked with the office and they submitted it twice.  Two days later it still stated missing PA information.  I went by the office again and noticed they had only completed the New Innovator Drug Form and NOT the PA with it.  The nurse completed the PA with me there and 4 hours later it was approved.  
Yvonne Drew-Alvarez <Yvonne.Drew-Alvarez@insmed.com>
2025-09-19 07:02 PM
I've had success with Tricare when both PA and new innovator drug form are sent in together. I've not had a HCP office call in a Tricare PA yet.

Humana Medicare Adv. J47.9 was denied. Heads up team ... one of my office's patients got an "approval" letter from UHC/Optum Med D but upon further reading of the letter it is approving a "temporary fill" for a 30 day supply. It went thru as 'no PA required' on the pharmacy's end but now it does indeed require a PA/Formulary Exception request. if anyone would like to look at the letter it is the most recent attachment in the case for insmed patient id 10304643
Jordana Weiss <jordana.weiss@insmed.com>
2025-10-01 02:11 PM
Michelle Miles you bring up an excellent point that we need to track and mark the patients when we hear that no PA is required because there's a good chance it could be a transitional fill. Tanya Frost <tanya.frost@insmed.com>
2025-10-07 11:21 PM
Has anyone had any experience with any skilled nursing facilities and push back regarding our limited distribution model?
Jill Budnik <jill.budnik@insmed.com>
2025-10-07 11:40 PM
SNF’s use what are called CDP’s (closed door pharmacies). Also, if the patient is in their skilled stay, the nursing home is required to pay for meds out of the lump sum they receive under pt’s medicare part A stay (similar to DRG’s for hospital stays). Meds are only billable to patient’s part D benefit (usually a Medicaid situation after skilled/part A stay days are up), when they are in a long term stay/nursing home is considered their permanent home. I did pharma sales in SNF’s for a long time - feel free to call me.
Madeline Kirincic <madeline.kirincic@insmed.com>

PantherRx Information: Hi Team, if a patient has not signed the EF panther does consider that as being consented (i.e verbal consent) per their governing rules and laws. Therefore when a status of PA info missing is indicated, They will be unable to share the information with us until we have received written consent (i.e signature). Please review the patient consent type in the form Consent section of patient account prior to an escalation for this matter. I hope this helps, Thank you!!

Michelle Miles <michelle.miles@insmed.com>
2025-10-28 02:55 PM
Hi FAM Nation! Just a head's up on the ESI IVR .... when the recording asks why you are calling or the reason for your call if you just say the word "Status" it should take you to the prompts for PA status. Sometimes it's sensitive to any other words offered, so the PAL team concluded last year that just saying the one word "Status" is the best way to get through. I hope this helps someone!

TRICARE The Department of Defense’s health insurance program.Covers active‑duty service members, retirees, and their families.Works like a traditional health plan with networks, referrals, and regional contractors. TriWest Healthcare Alliance A private company that administers VA community care.Serves veterans, not active‑duty families.Runs the Community Care Network (CCN) in certain regions for the VA.

Melissa Polvi <melissa.polvi@insmed.com>

Hi, with my VA patients. The HCP needs to escribe to the VA pharmacy. Call the pharmacy, get escription address, get a contact and get the Fax number, fax additional documentation.  Ask the contact what else they will need going forward to get the medication dispensed. One of my VA patients needed a Pulm consult through the VA, the other 4 just needed it escribed and accompanying documentation.
Message edited: Tanya Frost <tanya.frost@insmed.com>
2026-01-09 06:45 PM
Also make sure you have the office call the VA pharmacy to confirm receipt of all informatoin because sometimes they don't receive it, dont see it, and sometimes they say they dont have the information. This helps the office confirm that the VA did receive the documentation etc.
